NATIONAL SURVEY OF FAMILY GROWTH, CYCLE III
PUBLIC USE TAPE

Introduction

Fieldwork for the National Survey of Family Growth (NSFG), Cycle III was
conducted in 1982 by Westat, Inc., under contract with the National Center for
Health Statistics. Interviews were conducted by professional female
interviewers in respondents' homes with 7,969 women 15-44 years of age
regardless of marital status. This represents a departure from Cycles I and
II, from which never married childless women were excluded.

The data from the NSFG are used by the National Center for Health Statistics
as the basis for a series of reports on fertility, family formation,
contraception and related issues, and by contributing agencies for their own
research programs.

To make the data more widely available, a standardized micro-data tape has
been prepared for distribution. This: Tape Documentation Manual contains a
detailed description of the data tape.

The tape consists of the raw data for the respondent and interval files, and a
set of computer generated variables for each. The respondent file includes a
wide range of background characteristics, a number of measures of fertility
and contraception, measures of fecundity and birth expectations, use of family
planning services, and marital history. The interval file contains detailed
data from the pregnancy histories.

The staff of the NSFG will assist users as much as possible within the
constraints of time and staff availability.
Publications from the National Survey of Family Growth, Cycle III

The National Center for Health Statistics has planned reports from the NSFG,
Cycle III, on the following subjects: Contraceptive use; use of services for
family planning and infertility; number, timing and wantedness of births;
fecundity impairments including infertility; formation and disruption of
marital and non-marital unions; and the use effectiveness of contraceptives.

Individuals and educational institutions may obtain single copies of
publications free of charge by writing:

Scientific and Technical Information Branch
National Center for Health Statistics

3700 East-West Highway

Hyattsville, Maryland 20782

A list of NSFG publications was published in the Spring 1983 issue’ of
Population Index: "A Bibliography of Publications Based on the U.S. National
Survey of Family Growth."

A current listing of NSFG publications and copies of the Cycle III
questionnaire may be obtained by writing the Family Growth Survey Branch,

Pogson of Vital Statistics, NCHS, 3700 East-West Highway, Hyattsville, MD
Editing of the Data Before Imputation

The data were manually edited in the field by the interviewers' supervisors,
and by coders in the home office of Westat, Inc., the fieldwork contractor.
Over 220 pages of computer edits, all of them reviewed by the Family Growth
Survey Branch (FGSB) and the Computer Applications Staff (CAS) of the Division
of Vital Statistics (DVS), NCHS, were also conducted by the contractor before
the data tape was delivered to NCHS. Some of these edits were repeated by
NCHS to check the accuracy of the contractor's editing. Additional computer
edits for acceptable codes and internal consistency of both the raw data and
the computer generated variables ("recodes") were conducted by CAS and the
FGSB staff before imputation.

Missing and inconsistent data items were resolved through data retrieval in
the early stages of editing, and by examination of related items in the
questionnaire in the later stages. As far as possible, inconsistencies in the
recodes were resolved on a case-by-case basis, based on related data. If
inconsistencies could not be resolved, the case was assigned as missing for
the recode, and the value was imputed. Information on data-cleaning for
specific variables is included in the detailed recode specifications (Appendix
1), where relevant.
Missing Data

Data are sometimes missing for particular items, because the respondent would
not or could not answer the question, or because the interviewer mistakenly
skipped the question. For basic data (as opposed to "recodes") refusals and
"don't know" responses were coded "7" or "8" respectively. If it was not
useful for analytical purposes to code those responses, or when the datum was
missing due to interviewer error or other reasons, the item was considered
"not ascertained", and coded "9". For computer generated variables
("recodes") all missing data were grouped into one “not ascertained" code (9).

For a few items, data not obtained in the interview were imputed by the
contractor. For instance, if the datum sought was the month in which an event
occurred, but the respondent could remember only that it was "winter", the
month was imputed as “January"; similarly, if the day of the month was not
known, it was imputed as the 15th. For a very few respondents, race and age
were also imputed, because those data were necessary to compute weights.
Imputation

For the majority of the recodes, values were imputed for cases with missing
values. All imputed variables were "flagged." Recodes are listed in the
Respondent and Interval File Indexes, along with tape locations for imputation
flags for recodes which were imputed. This section describes how the
imputation was done.

A. Imputed variables

About 120 variables in the respondent file and 2 in the interval file were
imputed. The purpose of imputation was to allow NCHS to produce internally
consistent national estimates in its pre-planned Advance Data and Series 23
reports. The imputed variables include social and economic characteristics,
past and current contraceptive use, family planning services, fecundity,
fertility and marital history. We recommend that tape users use the imputed
variables for most analyses. Results using these variables should be
identical to results in NCHS reports using final (not preliminary) data.

B. Imputation procedures

For most variables, the level of missing data was very low (usually under 2
percent). A hot-deck imputation procedure with a standard matrix was used.
Exhibit 1 shows an example variable (VAR 165). The imputation matrix contains
4 marital status categories: never married and never had intercourse, never
married and ever had intercourse, currently married, and formerly married.
These are crossed with 2 race groups (black and other) and 3 age categories,
making a 24-cell matrix.

For each recode, the imputation specifications include initial values for each
cell. The specifications also contain the code categories, any special
instructions (e.g. in the exhibit, "Donor cases should contain non-blank
values"), and (in Exhibit 2) edit specifications for the imputed values.

All cases with imputed values that did not pass the edits were printed out,
and looked at individually to determine whether (1) the case was imputed
correctly; (2) the edit specifications were working as intended; or (3) the
case was a special situation (or "override"). Specifications were revised
extensively at this stage, and the process was repeated several times, until
the number of cases failing the edits was manageable (i.e., a few hundred).
Cases were again looked at individually, and those that did not involve
special situations ("overrides") were assigned individually. The process was
repeated until all cases in the entire file passed all the edits for all the
"recoded" variables.

C. Special imputation procedures

More refined imputation procedures were required for two variables: poverty
level income and date of first intercourse.

Poverty level income

This is the total family income expressed as a percentage of the poverty level
income for a family of that size. The level of missing data on this variable

was quite high. Since it is needed for many policy-related analyses, a
special imputation procedure was devised for it.

Initially, many cases which reported receiving food stamps, AFDC, or general
assitance had relatively high imputed incomes. To correct for this, the
imputation matrix was re-run separately for two groups: (1) those who received
income from food stamps, AFDC, or general assistance, and (2) those who did
not. The latter group had higher incomes than the former group. The data on
income after imputation compare favorably to that for women 16-44 from the
1982 current Population Survey, as reported in Current Population Reports,
Series P-60, No. 140, "Money Income and Poverty Status of Families and Persons
in the U.S.: 1982," table 17.

Date of First Intercourse

This variable was imputed differently for 3 groups of respondents, as shown in
Exhibits 3,4, and 5:

(1) Never married childless women
(2) Women with 1 or more births
(3) Ever married childless women

(1) For never married childless women who were sexually active, age at first
intercourse was imputed, and the date was calculated from the age. The
imputed date must be later than the date of menarche, before the date of
interview and before first conception, if any (See exhibit 3).

(2) For women with 1 or more births, the interval in months from date of first
intercourse to date of first birth was imputed, and the date of first
intercourse was obtained by subtracting the imputed interval from the date of
first birth. The imputed date of first intercourse must be greater than or
equal to the date of menarche, and less than or equal to the date of
interview, date of first conception, first birth, and first marriage if any
(Exhibit 4).

(3) For ever married childless women the interval in months from date of first
intercourse to date of first marriage was imputed, and the date of first
intercourse was obtained by subtraction. The imputed date must be greater
than or equal to the date of menarche and less than or equal to the dates of
interview, first marriage, and first conception (Exhibit 5).

Cases that failed these edits were run through the imputation program several
more times with different donor cases. If they still failed the edits, they
were examined individually and assigned dates that satisfied these conditions.

Data Quality

As indicated in the previous sections, the FGSB and CAS of DVS have directed
considerable effort toward cleaning the data, and producing a consistent data
tape. Inconsistencies may remain, especially among data not related to the
recodes. For several recodes there are apparent inconsistencies when the
marginals are compared with one another, that are the result of using
different definitions for similar concepts. For example:

1. A strict, narrow criterion based on question D-7 is used in defining
the contraceptive intent of sterilization in VAR 160. As a result, fewer
women are classified as sterilized for contraceptive reasons than are
categorized as ever having used female sterilization in VAR 136.

2. Variable 136 (TL 1067) and TL 342, Ever-use of female sterilization,
include a few hysterectomies that women reported as contraceptive in questions
D-7 and D-8. Researchers who wish to limit ever-use of female sterilization
to women who had tubal ligations may do so by using TL 388 or 407.

3. Never married and formerly married women whose partners are using
vasectomy are classified as contraceptively sterile in VAR 160 and as fecund
in VAR 360.

4. Twenty respondents for whom VAR 136=2 (Never used female
sterilization) are classified as currently using female sterilization as their

current method (VAR 160=01) because they had a hysterectomy and answered yes
to question D-7.

5. The definitions of "fecund" in VAR 310 and VAR 360 differ. (See
recode specifications, Appendix 1).

6. Throughout, there are 1195 women for whom certain questions are
inapplicable because they have never had intercourse, or they are never
married and have not had intercourse since menarche. They are classified as
follows on VAR 160: 1188 women have never had intercourse; 2 have not had
intercourse since menarche, and five (never-married) have never had
intercourse and are classified as non-surgically sterile for a total of 1195.

7. In VAR 170, most recent contraceptive method, a total of 1244 women
are classified "Never had intercourse, or under 25 and had intercourse only
once, or has not had intercourse since first menstrual period." The sum of
those three separate categories, including the five sterile women mentioned in
point 5, is 1240 for VAR 160. The difference of 4 includes one respondent,
younger than 25 who had intercourse only once and who was currently pregnant;
and three ever-married women who had never had a menstrual period.

In these cases, as with all the computer generated variables, reference to the
detailed specifications (Appendix 1) for clarification is recommended.

Evaluation of selected data items by the FGSB has identified a few items which
are of questionable quality and should be analyzed with caution. These
include pregnancies ending in induced abortions, income and date of first
intercourse (after menarche).
17

Abortions

As in the past in surveys of this kind, abortions were underreported by NSFG
respondents. The number of induced abortions in the 5 years before the survey
represents about 47 percent of the number reported during 1977-1981 by the
Alan Guttmacher Institute (AGI). The AGI data are based on national surveys
of all known providers of abortion services, including public and private
hospitals, clinics and private physicians. Underreporting is more severe
among non-white women than white women. The number reported by non-white
women represents 25 percent of the AGI figure for non-white women, compared
with 57 percent when NSFG data are compared to AGI data for white women.

With due allowance for samping variability, estimates of numbers of live
births and of miscarriages and stillbirths correspond fairly well to
information from other data sources. It appears that, rather than
misreporting pregnancy outcomes, the majority of the underreporting is the
result of women not reporting at all the pregnancies that ended in abortions.

Income

The primary problem with this variable is the level of missing data. See
section on imputation.

Date of First Intercourse

Data on the timing of first sexual intercourse are located in TL 190-196 and
in the computer-generated variable VAR 540. They were also used to compute
VAR 965, VAR 510 and VAR 511. This information was missing for 7 percent of
cases, and inconsistent with other information for an additional 4 percent of
cases. Inconsistencies included dates of first intercourse that were before
the first menstrual period (rather than after the first menstrual period,
which was the date requested), after the date the first pregnancy began, or
after the date of first marriage. Inconsistent cases were examined on a case-
by=-case basis and one of the following steps was taken:

0 the basic data in TL 190-196 were assigned the not ascertained code
and later imputed, when the inconsistency was large (6 or more
months). For these cases, TL 190-196 is coded as missing and VAR
540 contains an imputed value (141 cases).

o the basic data and VAR 540 were adjusted the minimum amount necessary
to achieve consistency, and both were flagged as estimates with a 9 in
the first position of the field, when the inconsistency was less than
6 months. These adjustments are detailed in the specifications for
VAR 540 (150 cases).

o the basic data and VAR 540 were left as is when other information in
the questionnaire corroborated the "inconsistent" data.

Because of these adjustments, these data should not be used to produce
estimates of pregnancies resulting from first intercourse. Such estimates
will be too high.
18

Sample Design, Estimation Procedures and Variance Estimation

A complete draft report on sample design, estimation procedures and variance
estimation is included in Appendix 2. The following briefly summarizes those
topics.

A. Sample Design

Cycle III of the National Survey of Family Growth was designed to represerit
all women aged 15 to 44 in the noninstitutional population of the conterminous
United States. The sample design for Cycle III was a five stage area
probability design that incorporated oversamples of black ~and teenage women
and a supplementary sample of women living in college dormitories and
sororities.

The counties and independent cities in the conterminous United States were
combined to form a frame of primary sampling units (PSU's). During the first
stage of the sampling process, which involved extensive stratification, 79
PSU's were chosen from this frame. Census block groups or enumeration
districts (ED's) were then identified within each of the selected PSU's during
the second stage, ED's were stratified according to the percent of their
population that was black, and a systematic sample was drawn. The rate at
which ED's were sampled varied from one stratum to the next in order to
produce the desired racial composition in the final sample of women.

In the third stage, area segments (groups of houses or apartments) within
sample ED's were identified and one segment was selected randomly from each
ED. In the fourth stage, households within sample segments were selected. In
segments from ED's with a 10-percent or greater black population, black
households were selected at a higher rate than other households. In the fifth
stage of sampling, women were selected from eligible households. In
households containing at least one eligible woman, the selection rate depended
on the number of eligible women in the household, stratum, age, race, and
marital status; no more than one woman was selected from any household.

B. Estimation Procedures

All analyses undertaken with these data should be "weighted." That is, in
order to produce unbiased estimates for the entire population of eligible
women in the conterminous United States, the sample data must be inflated to
the level of the population from which the sample was drawn. Estimates using
unweighted data will be incorrect, because of the oversampling of black and
teenage women. The inflation factor, or weight, for each woman is the product
of three types of adjustments.

(1) Inflation by the reciprocal of the probabilities of selection.--The
weight or probability of selection for each woman is the product of the
reciprocals of the probabilities of selecting (1) the primary sampling unit,
(2) the enumeration district, (3) the segment, (4) the household, and (5) the
eligible sample person.

(2) Nonresponse adjustment.--Each sample weight was adjusted for nonresponse
to the Household Screener (screener nonresponse) and nonresponse to the

19

detailed NSFG questionnaire (interview nonresponse). Adjustment for household
screener nonresponse was achieved by imputing to nonresponding households the
characteristics of responding households in the same primary sampling unit and
stratum. Adjustment for interview nonresponse was achieved by imputing to
nonresponding women the characteristics of responding women in the same age-
race-marital status class and primary sampling unit. Response to the
household screener was 95.1 percent; response to the interview was 83.5
percent, yielding a-combined response rate of 79.4 percent.

(3) Poststratification by age and race.--The nonresponse adjusted weight for
each respondent was then multiplied by a poststratification adjustment factor
determined by the woman's age, race and marital status. Adjustment factors
were equal to independent control totals for October 1982, the approximate
midpoint of data collection. The control totals were based on data from the
Current Population Survey (CPS) conducted by the U.S. Bureau of the Census,
interpolated to the midpoint of interviewing.

C. Variance Estimation

Since the weighted statistics computed from these data are based on a sample,
they will differ somewhat from the statistics that would result if a complete
census had been taken of all 54 million women represented by the NSFG. The
differences that might result by chance because only a sample of the full
population is surveyed are measured by the standard error. The chances are
about 65 out of 100 that a sample estimate would differ from a complete count
by less than one standard error, and about 95 out of 100 that a sample
estimate would differ by less than two standard errors.

Users are cautioned that tables of standard errors based on assumptions of
simple random sampling do not accurately estimate standard errors for the
NSFG, given the complex multistage design of the sample. .

Standard errors for the NSFG were estimated using the balanced half-sample
replication method. Because it would be prohibitively expensive to estimate,
and cumbersome to publish a standard error for each percent or other statistic
by this technique, standard errors were computed for selected statistics and
population sub-groups that were chosen to represent a broad range of
demographic characteristics and a wide variation in the size of the estimates
themselves. Curves were then fitted to the relative standard error estimates
for subgroups according to the model

RSE(N') = (A+B/N')%2

where N' is the number of women, and A & B are the parameters whose estimates
determine the shape of the curves.

Equations for calculating standard errors and relative errors for aggregates
or percents are included in Appendix 2, along with a description of the
population subgroups used for curve-fitting, and the resultant parameters.
20

Inapplicable Questions

Not all questions were applicable to all respondents. If a question was not
applicable, the interviewer skipped to the next applicable question. With
certain exceptions noted in the tape documentation, tape positions are blank
for inapplicable questions. Some computer programs read a blank as a
nonnumeric character, but others read it as zero. Users should take care to
distinguish between blanks and zeroes in programs used with these data.

The meaning of statistics based on responses to a question will depend, of
course, on who was asked the question and who was not. Because of the rather
complex skip patterns used in the interview, it is often not apparent who was
asked a particular question. In the documentation, however, the description
of the inapplicable (blank) code category for a question includes a precise
listing of women who were not asked the question. In addition to consulting
that list, the sequence of questionnaire items including an item of interest
should be examined carefully to determine the implications of skip patterns
for interpreting responses.

The documentation for the computer generated variables ("recodes")also
provides brief descriptions of inapplicable categories. The more complete
definitions of the inapplicable categories contained in the recode
specifications also should be examined.
21
Date Codes

During the interview, dates of events were obtained either as the year alone,
as month and year, or as day, month, and year. For this file, year and month
were converted to "century months" by multiplying the year by 12 and adding
the number of the month, where January = 1, February = 2, and so on. For
instance, the century month code for February 1959 is (59 x 12) + 2 = 710.
The century month form is more convenient for computing an interval between
dates. (Note: All century month dates occupy a four column field.) Days of
the month were deleted from the file.

The century month codes from 301 through 1000 are shown in the array in the
following pages with the years from 1925 through 1983 on the vertical axis and
the months on the horizontal axis. The code for a given month and year can be
found by reading across the line for the appropriate year to the column headed
by the appropriate month.

In cases where the question was inapplicable the position has four blanks.

In the basic data file, if the date was refused, the code is 9797; if the date
of the event was not known, the date code is 9898; other missing values are
coded 9999.

In a few instances, 9696 is used to indicate a response other than a date.
For example, in question A-8, concerning the date of first menstrual period,
respondents whose periods had not yet started are coded 9696. All such cases
are clearly indicated in the documentation.

In cases where the year was given but the month was not known, in the basic
data, the first digit of the date code is a 9 to indicate that the date is an
estimate. The remaining three digits are equal to the century month
corresponding to the month of June in the year given. If a year and a season
were given, the month was imputed by Westat as indicated in the section on
missing data. In those cases, the dates are not flagged as estimates.

With the exception of VAR 540 and VAR 606, estimated century months are not
flagged in the computer generated variables. Estimated dates for recodes
other than VAR 540 and VAR 606 may be identified by comparing them with the
raw data items from which they were constructed.

Occupational Classification

All occupations are classified according to the 3-digit codes used in the 1980
U.S. Census. A copy of the occupational classification system is attached for
reference.
Occupational Classification System

25

Equivalent numeric codes follow the alphabetic code. Either code may be used, depending on the processing method. Numbers in paren-
theses following the occupation categories are the 1977 Standard Occupational Classification code equivalents. The abbreviation “‘pt”
means “‘part”™ and ‘’n.e.c:” means “not elsewhere classified.”

33

Organization of the Data

The data file consists of 21,737 records, of which 7,969 are respondent
records (one record for each sample women) and 13,768 are interval records
(one record for each closed pregnancy interval reported by sample women).

The respondent records are arranged in ascending order by identification
number. This five digit I.D. number appears in locations 1494-1498 on both
respondent and interval records. Following each respondent record are the
interval records (if any) pertaining to that respondent, arranged in ascending
order by pregnancy number. In tape location 1499-1500 of each record,
respondent or interval, is a "record type" code: code 00 identifies a
respondent record, and code 01 to 17 identifies the first through the 17th
interVal record for each respondent.

All records, respondent or interval, are the same length, 1500 positions.
Thus, the file consists of fixed length records of two types, hierarchically
arranged. The file can be processed by software packages which assume fixed
length records of one type (e.g., SPSS) by selecting either respondent or
interval records (not both) using the record type code; however, with such a
package only records of one type can be processed on each pass over the data.
Software packages which can. process files with records of two or more types
(e.g., Crosstabs or TPL) can process this file and relate data from both
interval and respondent records on the same pass. A file of fixed length
records of one type containing the information from both the respondent and
interval records can be created from this file. Each record would consist of
the respondent information, followed by information for each of a woman's
intervals, followed by enough blank spaces to make each record as long as that
of the respondent who had the largest number of intervals. Each record would
be very long but the file could be processed by an SPSS-type system, and
interval and respondent data could be related on the same pass.

A. Respondent Record

The tape layout of the respondent file is shown in outline form on page 36.
The information in tape locations 11-821 is arranged on the tape as it
appears on the interview schedule. The arrangement is generally by topical
areas; but to improve the flow of the interview, items on similar topics
were sometimes separated. Note also that some questions were skipped for
some women, depending on their answers to earlier questions, and that
slightly different forms of the interview schedule were used for women age
25 and older and for women under age 25. The user should examine the
sequence of questions in which the item of interest occurs to determine
which pane women were asked the questions, and in which form the question
was asked.

Tape positions 822 to 840, and 847 to 855 are blank; the date of interview
is given in positions 841 to 846. The computer generated variables
("recodes") included on the respondent file are located in positions 1001-

1295, and 1431-1464. Imputation flags for the respondent recodes are found
in locations 1431-1464.
B.

C.

34

Interval Record

As shown in the Interval File Tape Layout outline (p. 37), positions 11-277
contain pregnancy interval information, arranged as it appeared in the
interview schedule. Computer generated variables ("recodes") and
imputation flags appear in positions 301-325. Except for the final seven
positions containing the I.D. number and record type, the remainder of the
interval record is blank, so that the interval records are equal in length
to the respondent records, a requirement of some software packages.

Weight

Because the NSFG sample is complex, and includes oversamples of certain
population subgroups, population estimates must be based on weighted data.
The public use tape includes two weights.

The nonresponse adjusted weight was calculated by inflating the inverse of
the probability of selection for a given case by a factor dependent on the
level of nonresponse among sample cases in the same locality and with the
same age, race and marital status. This weight should be used only for
methodological studies of the effects of post-stratification.

The final post-stratified weight is used in official NCHS reports and its
use will allow the user to replicate results in the official reports. It
adjusts for (1) the probability of selection, (2) non-response, and (3)
post-stratification to independent population estimates by age and race,
based on the Census Bureau's Current Population Survey. We recomménd that
users of this tape use this weight for all their analyses, except when they
are studying the effects of post-stratification.

- Sampling and Variance Estimation Variables

As noted, the NSFG is based on a complex sample design. Therefore, the
standard errors of NSFG statistics are larger than those that would be
obtained through simple random sampling. Appendix 2 describes the sample
design in detail and gives formulae for estimating standard errors for the
main types of statistics produced from NSFG data.

Most researchers using this tape will find it convenient to use the
standard error formulae and estimates given in Appendix 2. For tape users
who wish to calculate their own standard errors with any of the techniques
for estimating variances for complex sample designs, several items are
included on the tape. The items are:

1. Pair number (TL 993) and Pseudostratum (TL 991-992): Pseudostratum
describes the 37 strata into which the primary sampling units (PSUs) were
paired, and pair number indicates the first and second members of each
pair. These variables were used by NCHS to estimate variances (see
Appendix 2), and should be usable by most researchers calculating their own
variances.
35

2. PSU code (TL 989-990) is a two digit number from 1 to 79. The original
3-digit PSU numbers were recoded to protect the confidentiality of survey
respondents. The SSU number (TL 4-6) distinguishes the secondary sampling
units (or SSUs) within each PSU. The first digit of the SSU number.
indicates stratum, as defined in the second stage selection of enumeration
districts. Stratum ranges from 1 to 5, with strata 1 to 4 including
households in EDs with varying percents black, and stratum 5 including
dormitory residents (see Appendix 2). These variables may not be used for
small area estimation. We recommend they be used for variance estimation,

only if the pair number and pseudostratum are inappropriate to the user's
needs.

Key to Documentation

The following two sections document the contents of the respondent and the
interval files. The first part of each section consists of an index listing
the questionnaire items and the computer generated variables ("recodes") along
with the tape location numbers for each item. The list of recodes also shows
variable number, the tape location number of the recode imputation flag, and a
mnemonic variable label. The index is followed by documentation of the tape
contents.

The first part of the documentation for the respondent and interval files is
adapted from the documentation of the "raw" data file supplied by Westat,
Inc., the fieldwork contractor. The data were originally keypunched in a deck
(record) and column format, and the record and column numbers have been
retained in the documentation; the record number is shown on each page, and
the column numbers are shown next to the item description.

The tape location number shows the physical location of the datum on the data
file. Both the column number (from the initial deck and column format) and
the tape location appear under the heading "Column Number(s)." The tape
location number appears directly above the column number.

For each item the documentation includes the question name or number, the
question asked of the respondent, the numerical categories into which
responses were coded, and detailed category descriptions.

The documentation also shows the numbers of respondents in each category of
the item, separately for black women, white women, other women and women of
all races combined. These marginal distributions are raw unweighted counts of
sample women or pregnancy intervals; they are not estimates of numbers or
distributions for the population represented by the sample. To make such
estimates, sample cases must be weighted. The unweighted marginal
distributions are provided only as a guide to the analyst, who often wants to
Know in advance if the number of cases in a particular category is large
enough to sustain a particular type of analysis.

The second part of the documentation contains the recodes based on
specifications prepared by the Family Growth Survey Branch staff and
programmed by the Computer Applications Staff. (Appendix 1 contains the recode
specifications. )

The recodes are grouped by topical area, and each set designated by a series
number, as follows:

General Characteristics Series 900
Contraceptive Use Series 100
Family Planning Services Series 200
Fecundity & Infertility Series 300
Fertility Series 400
Sexual Intercourse & Marriage Series 500
First Pregnancy Series 600

Instead of question number, the recode documentation lists a mnemonic variable
label. Also, record and column numbers are excluded; only the tape location
number is given under "Column Number(s)." Other parts of the recode
documentation are the same as that for the basic data.

METHOD USE FOR MONTH OF INTERVIEW - FOURTH DIGIT

PRIORITY GROUP A INCLUDES FEMALE STERILIZATION,
MALE STERILIZATION, PILL , AND IUD. PRIORITY
GROUP B INCLUDES DIAPHRAGM AND CONDOM. PRIORITY
GROUP C INCLUDES ALL OTHER METHODS.

BLANK = INAPPLICABLE , R‘S PERIOD HAVE NOT
STARTED (CODED 9696, COLS. 62 - 65,
CARD 02 OR CODED 96, COLS. 66-67,
CARD 02), OR R NEVER HAD INTERCOURSE
(CODED 2 IN COL. 30, CARD 03), OR R
HAD INTERCOURSE BUT NOT SINCE FIRST
MENSTRUAL PERIOD (CODED 9595 , COLS.
33-36, CARD 08), OR R NEVER PREGNANT
AND NEVER HAD SECOND INTERCOURSE
(CODED 00, COLS. 76-77, CARD O3 AND
CODED O06 , COL. 40, CARD 08)

fe) = NO ADDITIONAL METHODS, I.E., NO
METHODS OR ONE OR TWO METHODS USED IN
MONTH

1 = THREE METHODS , THIRD METHOD IN
PRIORITY GROUP A

2 = THREE METHODS , THIRD METHOD IN
PRIORITY GROUP B

3 = THREE METHODS , THIRD METHOD IN
PRIORITY GROUP C

4 = FOUR METHODS , BOTH ADDITIONAL
METHODS IN GROUP A

5 = FOUR METHODS , BOTH ADDITIONAL
METHODS IN GROUP B

6 = FOUR METHODS , BOTH ADDITIONAL
METHODS IN GROUP C .

7 = FOUR METHODS , ONE ADDITIONAL METHOD
IN GROUP A AND ONE IN GROUP B

(OR YOUR HUSBAND) HAD TO HELP YOU (BECOME

MENTION

BLANK ‘= INAPPLICABLE , NEITHER R NOR HUSBAND
HAVE VISITED A DOCTOR OR CLINIC TO
TALK ABOUT WAYS TO BECOME PRIEGNANT OR
HELP PREVENT A MISCARRIAGE (CODED 2
IN E-1, COL. 18 AND E-2, COL. 19) ,
OR R DID NOT RECEIVE ANY ADVICE OR
TREATMENT (CODED 2 IN E-3, COL. 20),
OR RESPONDENT HAS NOT HAD
INTERCOURSE, (CODED 2, COL. 30, CARD
03) , OR R HAS HAD INTERCOURSE BUT
NOT SINCE FIRST MENSTRUAL PERIOD
(CODED 9595, COLS. 33-36, CARD 08)

01 = FERTILITY DRUGS TO STIMULATE
OVULATION

02° = SURGERY OR OTHER TREATMENT TO OPEN
BLOCKED TUBES, HAVING TUBES "BLOWN
OPEN"

O3 = SURGERY OR OTHER TREATMENT TO CORRECT

POSITION OF UTERUS (WOMB)

04 = ARTIFICIAL INSEMINATION

05 = ADVICE ON HOW TO TIME INTERCCIURSE TO
INCREASE CHANCE OF PREGNANCY

o6 = ADVICE ON HOW TO EXECUTE INTERCOURSE

TO INCREASE CHANCE OF PREGNANCY
07 = LIFE STYLE CHANGES (£.G., CHANGE

DIET, GET ADDITIONAL REST, REDUCE

STRESS , ETC.), OR GENERAL HEALTH

ADVICE

08 = TESTS OR EXAMINATION (EXCEPT GENERAL
PHYSICAL EXAM) TO FIND OUT WHAT WAS
WRONG

os = NO TREATMENT OR ADVICE GIVEN, “JUST
WAIT AND SEE"

11 = ADVISED TO STOP USING BIRTH CONTROL

12 = ADVICE ABOUT REVERSING STERILIZING
PROCEDURE (TUBAL LIGATION)

13 = ADVISED TO GO ON PILL TO REGULATE
CYCLE

14 = ADVISED TO GO OFF PILL IN ORDER TO
BECOME PREGNANT

415 = DOCTOR SAID IT WAS IMPOSSIBLE FOR HER
TO BECOME PREGNANT

16 = DOCTOR SAID THAT THIS IS A GOOD TIME
TO BECOME PREGNANT

17 = MISCELLANEOUS ADVICE ON HOW TO BECOME
PREGNANT, NO TREATMENT RECEIVED

18 * = RESPONDENT HAD TUBAL LIGATION
REVERSED

20 = LIFE STYLE CHANGES (E£.G., BED REST,

STAY OFF FEET, RESTRICT ACTIVITY ,
ETC.), OR GENERAL HEALTH ADVICE

21 = DOCTOR ADVISED RESPONDENT TO USE
SPECIFIC METHOD OF BIRTH CONTROL OR
TO USE BIRTH CONTROL TO PREVENT
ANOTHER MISCARRIAGE

22 = DOCTOR ADVISED TO POSTPONE PREGNANCY
23 = MISCELLANEOUS ADVICE ON PREVENTION OF
- MISCARRIAGE, NO TREATMENT RECEIVED

24 = REFRAIN FROM INTERCOURSE

25 = DOCTOR RECOMMENDED SURGERY, BUT
RESPONDENT DID NOT HAVE IT DONE

26 = ADVICE OR CONSULTATION, UNSPECIFIED

“018 . DID YOU (OR ANY MEMBER OF YOUR FAMILY LIVING HERE)
mo, . RECEIVE INCOME IN THE PAST 12 MONTHS FROM ANY OF

APPENDIX 1

SPECIFICATIONS FOR COMPUTER GENERATED VARIABLES (''RECONES'')
ap +

APPENDIX 1.
SPECIFICATIONS FOR COMPUTER GENERATED VARIABLES ("RECODES")

This section contains the detailed specifications used to create the

computer generated variables ("recodes"). The basic data are usually referenced:
by record/column location (e.g., 2/17); data from recodes are referenced by the
tape location (TL).

Specifications for most variables relating to age at a particular event are
excluded. Unless otherwise specified, age variables are calculated as the

century month of the event minus the century month of birth, divided by 12.

Four column age variables have an implied decimal after the second column. If

an analyst wants to use age in completed years as a variable, the first two

columns of the variable should be used. The four column age variable should be used
for computing mean ages. Two-column age variables refer to age in completed years.

For certain groups of variables, such as ever-use of various contraceptive methods,
specifications differ only in the tape locations from which the raw data were taken.
To avoid repetition, the specification for one variable in the group is included,
with a list of the other variables which have been created similarly.

In the specifications that follow, a few cases were treated separately because the
respondent had never had a menstrual period and was not asked some sections of the
questionnaire.

If ever married and TL 55-58 = 9696 or TL 59-60 = 96 (3 cases),
and the variable is blank, then the following codes were assigned:

var 965=99

var 100=1

var 105=2

var 110 and 115=blank
var 120 to var 150 = 2

var 155 = 3

var 160 = 23

var 165 = blank

var 205 to var 298* = 9 or 99
var 310 =2

var 311 = 2 if currently married
var 312 = 9 if it falls through
var 315 =blank

var 320 =5

var 325 =blank
var 330 to var 340 =0

var 345 = 1
var 350 = 1
var 355 = blank
var 360 =3

var 370-372 = 99
var 510,511=999

*If R is 25 or older, leave 205-220 = Blank
var 519,520=9
var 540 = 9

If R is never married, under 18, and TL 55-58=9696 or
TL 59-60 =96(4 cases), then the following codes were assigned:

var 100 =2

var 105 to var 155 =blank
var 160 =23
var 205 to var 298 =blank
var 310,315,325 =blank
var 320 =5

var 330 to 340 =zero

var 345 = 1

var 350 = 1

var 355 = blank

var 360 =3

In addition, 9 respondents refused to answer whether they had ever

had sexual intercourse, at B-11. If TL 75=7(9 cases), var 100 to var 355

were set =9 or 99, and vars 519,520,540 = 9, if the variable was blank or missing.
Values for recodes were imputed for these cases.

GENERAL CHARACTERISTICS
VAR905 "Age at interview" (AGE)

Calculate as the difference between date of interview (TL 841-844)
and date of birth (TL 12-15) divided by 12 and truncated to an integer.

If the calculated age = 45, age was changed to 44.

NOTE: Eligibility for the interview was determined by age at the

date of the household screener. VAR905 and 906 are age at the date of the
extended interview, the 12 respondents who had their 45th birthdays

between the screener and interview dates are classified as being 44 years of age.

VAR910 "Race by observation" (RACEOBS)

Transferred from 20/18.
VAR911 "Self-reported race" (SRRACE)

If the respondent reports that:
she is black(25/32=3), SRRACE=1.
she is white(25/32=4), SRRACE=2.
she is some other race(25/32=1,2,5), SRRACE=3.

If 25 /32=7,8,0r 9, then use race by interviewer observation (20/18).
—

—

VAR915 "Marital status" (MARITAL)

Code marital status as reported on household screener. If marital status
changed between screener and interview, code marital status as of extended
interview. If R is currently not married but living with a partner
(informally married), code 2.

VAR916 "Formal Marital Status" (FMARITAL)

If the respondent is currently married, widowed, divorced,
or never married.(MARITAL EQ 1,3,4,6), then
FMARITAL = MARITAL.

If ‘the respondent is informally married or separated (MARITAL=2 or 5),
then: a

if she has never been formally married (VAR976 eq blank),
FMARITAL = 6 (never married)

if she has ever been formally married (VAR976 ne blank),
identify the outcome of her most recent formal marriage:
this is in

23/25 if 23/24 eq 2-9 (most recent marriage applicable
and not informal)

22/25 if 23/24 eq 1 or blank and 22/24 eq 2-9
21/25 if 23/24 and 22/24 eq 1 or blank and 21/24 eq 2-9

if the outcome of the most recent formal marriage is death
(code 3), FMARITAL = 3 (widowed)

if the outcome of the most recent formal marriage is divorce
(code 4), FMARITAL = 4 (divorced)

if the outcome of the most recent formal marriage is
separation (code 5) FMARITAL = 5 (separated).

VAR920 "Hispanic origin (old version)" (SPANISH1)

If the respondent mentioned that her ancestry or national

origin was Puerto Rican, Cuban, Mexican American, Central or
South American, or other Spanish (25/34-35 or 36-37 coded 8,9,10,
11 or 12), then she is Hispanic, code 1.

If the respondent did not mention any Hispanic origin (25/34-35
and 36-37 coded 1,2...7,13,14...18), then she is non-Hispanic, code 2.

VAR922 "Hispanic origin (new version)" (SPANISH2)

If the respondent's only reported national origin, or the
national origin which she says best describes her ancestry, is
Puerto Rican, Cuban, Mexican American, Central or South American,
or other Spanish (25/34-35 coded 8,9,10,11, or 12), then she is
Hispanic, code 1. .
4

If the respondent gives a non-Hispanic origin as her only
origin or that which best describes her ancestry (25/34-35 coded
1,2,..-6,7,13,14,...17, or 18), then she is non-Hispanic, code 2.

VAR925 "Geographic region" (REGION)

If the respondent lives in Maine, New Hampshire, Vermont,
Massachussetts, Rhode Island, Connecticut, New York, New Jersey,
or Pennsylvania, then she lives in the Northeast,

code 1.

If the respondent lives in Ohio, Indiana, Illinois, Michigan,
Wisconsin, Minnesota, Iowa, Missouri, North Dakota, South Dakota,
Nebraska, or Kansas, then she lives in the north central region, code 2.

If the respondent lives in Delaware, Maryland, D.C., Virginia,
West Virginia, North Carolina, South Carolina, Georgia, Florida,
Kentucky, Tennessee, Alabama, Mississippi, Arkansas, Louisiana,
Oklahoma, or Texas, then she lives in the south, code 3.

If the respondent lives in Montana, Idaho, Wyoming, Colorado,
New Mexico, Arizona, Utah, Nevada, Washington, Oregon, California,
Alaska, or Hawaii, then she lives in the west, code 4.

VAR930 "Education (completed years of schooling)" (EDUCAT)

If the respondent completed the highest grade she attended
(20/41 coded 1), then her education is the highest grade she
attended (20/39-40).

If the respondent did not complete the highest grade she
attended (20/41 coded 2), her education is the grade below the
highest grade she attended (20/39-40 minus 1).

If the respondent had no formal schooling (20/39-40 coded 00),
then she completed no years of schooling, code 00.

If the respondent reported the highest grade she attended
(20/39-40 coded 1 to 18), but did not report whether or not she

had completed that grade (20/41 coded 7,8, or 9), then her education
is the highest grade she attended (20/39-40).

VAR935 "Religious affiliation" (RELIGION)
Transferred from 25/25.

VAR940 "Poverty level income" (POVERTY)

Poverty level income is the respondent's family income divided
by the official poverty threshold for a family the size of her
family, expressed as a percent. If the value is 998 or greater,
set = 998.

For this recode an exact family income is estimated by the
midpoint of the reported range of family income (26/27-28) as
follows: 01=2000, 02=3750, 03=5500, 04=6500, 05=7500, 06=8500,
07=9500, 08=10500, 09=11500, 10=12500, 11=14000, 12=16000, 13=18500,
14=22500, 15=30000, 16=42500, 17=55000.
5
The poverty thresholds for each family size are:

Family Size Threshold

1 4,901
2 6,281
3 7,693
4 9,862
5 11,684
6 13,207
7 15,036
8 ; 16,719
9 or more 19,698

The thresholds were published in: U.S. Bureau of the. Census, Current
Population Reports, Series P-60, No. 140, Money Income and Poverty Status of
Families and Persons in the U.S., 1982. Washington, D.C., July 1983.

VAR960 “Father's Education" (EDUCDAD)

The educational attainment of the respondent's father is in
20/25-26, codes 00-18.

then her father's education as reported on the parent questionnaire was used.

Based on 20/32-33, specifications correspond to VAR 960.
VAR970 "Years since first marriage" (MAR1/NOW)

If the respondent has never been married (02/18 coded 3 or 6),
then years since first marriage is not applicable, code blank.

If the respondent has been married once (20/51-52 coded 1)
and is currently married (02/18 coded 1 or 4), then the century
month of her first marriage is 20/53-56.

If the respondent is formerly married (02/18 coded 2 or 5) or
has been married more than once (20/51-52 coded 2,3,...7, or 8),
then the century month of her first marriage is 21/18-21.

If the month and year of the first marriage were given

(locations above coded 577 through 997), then the number of years
since first marriage is the difference between the century month of
interview completion (26/49-52) and the century month of first
marriage, divided by 12 and truncated to an integer.
6
Marriage refers to formal or informal marriage.

VAR975 “Century month of first marriage" (MAR1MO)

If the respondent has never been married (02/18 coded 3 or 6),
then century month of first marriage is not applicable, code blank.

If the respondent has been married once (20/51-52 coded 1)

and is currently married (02/18 coded 1 or 4), then the century
month of her first marriage is 20/53-56.

If the respondent is formerly married (02/18 coded 2 or 5) or
has been married more than once (20/51-52 coded 2,3,...7, or 8),
then the century month of her first marriage is 21/18-21.
Marriage refers to formal or informal marriage.

VAR976 "Century month of first formal marriage" (FMARIMO) —

If the respondent has never been formally married (20/59 and 21/24
and 22/24 and 23/24 all equal blank or 1), then century month
at first formal marriage is inapplicable, code BLANK.

If the respondent has ever formally married, code the century
month of marriage for the first marriage coded as formal (codes
2-9) in the following positions:

status was volunteered information.

VAR977 "Year of first marriage" (MARLYR)

If the respondent has never been married (02/18 coded 3 or 6),
then year of first marriage is not applicable, code blank.

If the respondent has been married once (20/51-52 coded 1)
and is currently married (02/18 coded 1 or 4), then the century
month of her first marriage is 20/53-56.

If the respondent is formerly married (02/18 coded 2 or 5) or
has been married more than once (20/51-52 coded 2,3,...7, or 8),
then the century month of her first marriage is 21/18-21.

If the month and year of the first marriage were given

(locations above coded 577 through 997), then the year of the first
marriage is the century month of the first marriage divided by 12 and
truncated to an integer. - |

VAR985 “Intentions for additional births" (INTENT)

If the respondent is sterile, or subfecund and intends

an operation or abortion if she does become pregnant

again(17/18 coded blank), OR if R says that she does

not intend to have a(nother)baby(17/18 coded 2), then : oo
she does not intend to have a(nother) baby, so INTENT=2.

If she says she intends to have a(nother) baby,
(17/18 coded 1), then she does. intend (more) children, -
so INTENT=1.

If R said that she didn't know, it was up to God,
etc(17/18 coded 8), or she and her husband disagree
(17/18 coded 3), then her intent is ‘unclear, SO
INTENT=3. ,

NOTE: For currently pregnant women INTENT refers to
intentions after the current pregnancy.

VAR165 “First source of current method" (MTHDSRC1)

If R has never used a method(14/32-34 coded blank), or

is not currently using a method(14/32-34 coded 996) or is

code in 14/37-38.

and first methods are the same), take the code in 14/29-30.

if she first obtained her current method from a

private doctor or medical service(14/29-30 or 14/37-38 coded
01), or hospital (14/29-30 or 14/37-38 coded 09), then
MTHDSRC1 was a private doctor, so MTHDSRC1=1.

if she obtained her method from a clinic(14/29-30 or
14/37-38 coded 02), then MTHDSRC1=2.

if she obtained her method from a store(14/29-30 or
14/37-38 coded 03 or 04), then MTHDSRC1=3.

if she obtained her method from some other source
(14/29-30 or 14/37-38 coded 05-08 or 10), then MTHDSRC1=4.

VAR170 "Most Recent Contraceptive Method" (RECENT)

If R is currently using a method(CONSTAT=01-14), then
RECENT=CONSTAT.

If R has never had intercourse(17/41 coded 2) or is under 25 and has only
had intercourse once(08/40 coded 6) or R has not had intercourse since first
menstrual period(08/33-36 coded 9595), then she did not use a method,

so RECENT=96.

If R has had intercourse but never used a method
(ANYMTHD=2), then RECENT=88.

If R has had intercourse at least twice and has
ever used a method(ANYMTHD=1), but is not currently
using a method(CONSTAT coded 20-29 or 32), then
RECENT is the most recent method in the open
interval(13/59-61 or 46-48 or 33-35 or 20-22

if 13/18 coded 1), or closed interval

(11/55-57 or 43-45 or 31-33 or 19-21 IF 11/18=1),or
(09/28-30 or 25-27 or 22-24 or 19-21 IF 9/18=1).

If the most recent method code is 009, 016-029, 616-629,
or 816-829, then RECENT=01.

If the most recent method is 010, 030-042, 630-642,
or 830-842, then RECENT=02.

If ANYMTHD=1 but 13/18=2 AND 11/18=2 and 09/18=2
for all intervals, then RECENT is the

code with the highest priority in 14/26-28.
(Determined according to the same rules as for
CONSTAT. )

If there is missing data for a method or interval,
go on to the previous interval; if data are missing
for all methods or intervals, then RECENT=99.

FAMILY PLANNING SERVICES

NOTE: By definition, non-medical counselors cannot provide medical family
planning services (items F. through J. in E.10, E.16, and E.21). Women
who reported a counselor as the only source of family planning service,
and the receipt of one or more medical services from that source were
reviewed individually. Inconsistencies were resolved based on other
16

information on the types of services received and data on sources
of contraceptives (section E and questions C.45-47).

VAR205 "Services used at the first family planning visit: got
a method" (FPLMTHD)

If the respondent was 25 years of age or older at interview, or
never had a family planning visit, or she never had intercourse
(19/42-43 coded blank), then services used at first visit is not
applicable, code blank.

If the respondent reported "getting a method of birth control"
at the first visit (19/42-43,44-45,46-47, or 48-49 coded 10),
then she got a method of birth control at her first visit, code 1.

If she reported getting a service at first visit, but did not
mention getting a method of birth control ((19/42-43,44-45,46-47, or
48-49 coded 1,2,3 ... 8, or 9) and (19/42-43,44-45,46-47, and 48-49
not coded 10)), then code 2.

VAR210 "Services used at the first family planning visit: other medical
(FP1OMED)

See VAR 205 for code blank.

If the respondent reported getting a medical service other than
getting a method of birth control (19/42-43,44-45,46-47 or 48-49 coded
6,7,8, or 9), then code l.

If the respondent did not mention getting a medical service other
than birth control (19/42-43,44-45,46-47, and 48-49 not coded 6,7,8
and 9) and did mention getting another service (19/42-43,44-45, 46-47,
or 48-49 coded 1,2,3,4,5 or 10), then code 2.

VAR215 "Services used at the first family planning visit: advice on
birth control" (FPIFPADV)

See VAR 205 for code blank.

If the respondent reported getting advice or counseling on
birth control (19/42-43,44-45,46-47, or 48-49 coded 5), then code 1.

If the respondent did not mention getting advice or counseling

on birth control (19/42-43,44-45,46-47 and 48-49 not coded 5) and

did mention getting some other service (19/42-43,44-45,46-47, or 48-49
coded 1,2,3,4,6,7,8,9 or 10), then code 2.

(FPLOADV )
See VAR 205 for code blank.

than advice or counseling on birth control (19/42-43,44-45, 46-47 or

VAR225 "Age at first use of family planning services" (FP1AGE)

If the respondent has never had a family planning visit or never
had intercourse(19/35-38 coded blank), then her age at first
visit is not applicable, code blank.

If the respondent gave the month and year of her first family
planning vist (19/35-38 coded 577 through 997), then her age at first
visit computed like other age variables.

If the respondent gave her age at first visit (19/39-40 coded 10
through 44) and her first visit was after her birthday (19/41 coded
2), then that (19/39-40) is her age at first visit.

If the respondent gave her age at first visit (19/39-40 coded 10
through 44) and her first visit was before her birthday (19/41 coded

1), then her age at first visit is her reported age at first visit
(19/39-40) minus 1.

VAR230 "First source of family planning services" (FP1SRC)

If the respondent was 25 years of age or older at interview
(02/18 coded 4,5, or 6), then source of first visit is not
applicable, code blank.

If the respondent was under 25 (02/18 coded 1,2, or 3), and

had not had a family planning visit or had never had intercourse
(19/50-51 coded blank), then she has not had a visit while sexually
active, code 6.

If the respondent's first family planning visit was to a private
doctor, private group practice, co-op or private clinic (19/50-51
coded 14 or 15), then the first source of services was private
medical services, code 1.

NOTE: Space was provided for coding up to two sources of family planning
services in 18/55-58. For cases listing more than two sources, clinics
were coded before private medical services; and clinics and private
medical services were coded before counselors. As a result, the sum of
visits to private doctors, clinics and counselors did not always sum to
the total number of visits (VAR 276) when more than two sources were
reported. If the sum of variables 270,272, and 274 did not equal VAR 276,
inconsistencies were resolved by looking at the individual questionnaire.

VAR293 "2nd kind of infertility service" (TREAT2)

See VAR 292 for code blank.

If there is only one 2-digit code in 18/21-28, then
the couple received just one type of treatment or advice,
so code 96.

If more than one 2-digit field in 18/21-24 is coded,

then TREAT2 is the code in 18/21-24 that

was NOT coded in Var 292, and is coded

according to the specifications for var 292. If there is
no code there, but there is one code in 18/25-28, then
TREAT2 is the code for var 294. If there are no codes

in 18/21-24 but there are two codes in 18/25-28, then
TREAT2 is the 2nd code for the husband's treatment--the
one that is NOT coded in var 294.

Thus, legal codes are:

Blank, 01-09(from var 292), 20-22(from var 294),96, and 99.

FECUNDITY AND INFERTILITY
VAR304 "Want or intend another birth" (WANTKID1)

Categories were tested in the order listed here.

If the respondent (or husband) is surgically sterile (16/19=1),
use 16/59; if the respondent's periods have not started yet
(16/62-63=04), use 17/18; if the respondent is nonsurgically
sterile for other reasons (16/19=2 and 16/62-63 NE 045, use 16/70;
if the respondent intends to have a sterilization operation or
abortion for health reasons (16/75=1 or 16/76=1 or 16/77=1), use
16/78; otherwise use 17/18.
25

"No" responses=1-2
"Yes" responses=3-5
Not ascertained=9

If R would NOT like to have another child even though it is
unlikely or imposible that she can (16/59 or 16/70 or 16/78=2),
WANTKID1=1.

If R does not intend to have another child or disagrees with
husband about it (17/18=2 or 3), then WANTKID1=2.

If R would like to have a child or another child even though it is
unt tke ly or impossible that she can (16/59 or 16/70 or 16/78=1), then
WANTKID1=3.

If R intends to have a baby or another baby (17/18=1), then WANTKID1=4.

If R says she doesn't know if she intends to have a child or that it
is up to God (16/59 or 16/70 or 16/78=8 or 17/18=8), then WANTKIDI=5.

If 16/59 or 16/70 or 16/78 or 17/18=9, then WANTKID1=9.

NOTE: This recode is not completely comparable to that in Cycle 2 because
of changes in the questionnaire. In Cycle 2, women who were
contraceptively sterile were not asked about wanting another baby, because
they were assumed NOT to want one. Thus, these women are asked the question
in Cycle 3, and this will tend to make the proportions wanting
a(nother) baby higher in Cycle 3 than they were in Cycle 2.

In addition, in Cycle 3 there is no question on whether a formerly

married R expects to remarry or reunite, as there was in Cycle 2. Formerly
married R's who did not expect to remarry or reunite were not asked about
wanting a(nother) baby, because they were assumed not to want a baby,

in Cycle 2. Thus, the results for formerly married women in Cycle 3

may differ from those in Cycle 2 because all are given the chance to answer
‘yes' in Cycle 3. This will also tend to increase the proportions

saying yes--that they want a(nother)baby.

VAR305 "Collapsed WANTKID" (WANTKID2)

If WANTKID1=1 or 2, then R does NOT want a
baby or another baby, so WANTKID2=1.

If WANTKID1=3,4, or 5, then R DOES want a baby
or another baby, so WANTKID2=2.

NOTE: WANTKID1 is not imputed;
WANTKID2 js.

VAR311 "Detailed Infertility status" (INFERT2)

If the respondent is never married or formerly married
then infertility is not applicable, so INFERTZ=BLANK.

If R or husband is surgically sterile for contraceptive
reasons(CONSTAT coded 01 or 02), then INFERT2=1.

If R or husband is surgically sterile for noncontraceptive
reasons(CONSTAT coded 25,26,27,28), then INFERT2=2.

If INFERT=2 and R has been infertile for 36 months or
more(during the 36 months of continuous marriage the
couple did not have a pregnancy and did not use
contraception), then INFERT2=3.

If INFERT=2 and she has been infertile for 24-35 months,
then INFERT2=4.

If INFERT=2 and she has been infertile for 12-23 months,
then INFERT2=5.

If 16/19 is coded 2 or CONSTAT=23, 24, or 29, and R has not
yet been classified infertile on INFERT2, then INFERT2=6.

If there is missing data, INFERT2=9.
All other respondents are fecund, INFERT2=7.
VAR312 "Number of Months Infertile" (NINFERT)

If the respondent is not currently married or not
infertile(INFERT not equal to 2), then NINFERT=blank.

If the respondent is currently married and infertile
(INFERT coded 2), then NINFERT is the number of months
that the couple has been infertile--that is, the number
of months they have been continuously married, have

not used any contraception, and did not become pregnant.
28

Thus, NINFERT is calculated by taking - for infertile respondents only -
the difference, in months, between:

current marriage and interview,
last method use and interview, and
last pregnancy termination and interview.

The smallest of these values will be the number of months infertile.
The range will be 12 to 360.

VAR315 "Age at Menarche" (MENARCHE)

If the respondent's periods have not started yet(02/64-65
coded 96 or 02/66-67 coded 96), then she does not yet
have an age at menarche, so MENARCHE=BLANK.

If the respondent is under 25(02/18 coded 1,2,or 3),

then her age at her first period is based on the century month
of the first period (02/62-65) and the century month of her birth.

If the respondent is 25 or older(02/18 coded 4,5,or 6),
or is under 25((02/18 coded 1,2, 3) AND did not report
the month and year of her first period(02/64-65 coded
97, 98, or 99)), then her age at her first period is her
reported age(02/66-67 IF 02/68 coded 2; or, 02/66-67
minus one if 02/68 is coded 1).

If Vasectomy(16/21 coded 4), STRLOPER=3.

If Other or unknown type(16/21 coded 1,5,7,8,9),
STRLOPER=4.

If there are two operations coded(16/20 coded 2-5), then:
If there is a male operation(16/21 coded 4 or 16/40
coded 4) AND a female operation(16/21 NE 4 or 16/40
NE 4)coded, then classify the female operation.

If there are 2 female operations or.2 male operations,

then classify the respondent according to the earlier

operation (compare the dates in 16/22-25 and 16/41-44),

VAR325 "Age at sterilization operation" (STERLAGE)

If the respondent or her current husband have not had a
sterilization operation(16/20 coded BLANK), then the
respondent does not have an age at sterilization, so
STERLAGE=BLANK.

If the respondent or her husband have had a total of one
sterilization operation(16/20 coded 1), then the age at
sterilization is the century month of the operation
(16/22-25) minus the woman's date of birth(02/19-24),
divided by 12.

If there are 2 sterilization operations(16/20 coded 2-5),

and one is female(16/21 coded 1,2,3,5,9 or 16/40 coded
1,2,3,5,9), and the other is male(16/21 coded 4 or
16/40 coded 4), then use the century month of the
woman's operation and her date of birth.
30

and both operations are male or both are female,
then use the date of the earlier operation and the
woman's date of birth.

NOTE: In 9 cases in which the current husband had a
vasectomy before marrying the respondent, STERLAGE reflects
the respondent's age at which she began being protected by the
vasectomy, rather than her age when the operation was performed.

VAR330 "Number of pregnancies" (PREGNUM)
Transferred from 3/76-77.

VAR331 "Number of completed pregnancies" (COMPREG)

This is the total number of completed pregnancies
the respondent has had--i.e., the number in 03/76-77,
minus one if the respondent is currently pregnant
(03/75=1).

VAR332 "Number of pregnancies excluding induced abortions" (SPONPREG)

SPONPREG is the total number of pregnancies a woman has had that ended

in anything other than abortion, and excluding current pregnancies
(03/76-77, minus one if 03/75=1), minus the number of pregnancies in which
Sacbetenes coded 2, or 04/21 is coded 2, or 04/22 is coded 2, or 04/23
is code .

VAR333 "Number of pregnancies ending in live birth" (LBPREGS)

Transferred from 3/69-70.

VAR335 "Number of pregnancies ending in spontaneous pregnancy loss"

(LOSSNUM)

This is the total number of pregnancies a woman has had

that ended in 1 or more pregnancy losses regardless of

any other outcomes of those pregnancies

(04/18-19 coded 3,4,or 10, or 04/21 coded 3 or 4,

OR 04/22 coded 3 or 4, OR 04/23 coded 3 or 4). (This is

not the same as 03/73-74 if she had a live birth and a
stillbirth from the same pregnancy, because only the primary
outcome is coded in the pregnancy totals in deck 3.)

VAR340 "Children ever born by C-section" (C-SECNUM)

This is the count of the number of live births by
Cesarian Section each respondent has had(04/24 coded 4).
It is applicable for every respondent; i.e., respondents
with no live births will have a value of zero.

VAR345 "Treated and/or hospitalized for PID?" (PIDTREAT)

If the respondent has never had intercourse or never
been treated (19/56 coded 2 or BLANK), then she has
never been treated, so PID=1.
aes

31

If the respondent has been treated but not hospitalized
(19/56 coded 1 AND 19/57 coded 1), then PID=2.

If the respondent has been both treated and hospitalized
(19/56 coded 1 AND 19/57 coded 2, 3, or 4), then PID=3.

If there is missing data on hospitalization (19/57 coded 9)
but R was treated (19/56 coded 1), then PID = 2.

VAR355 "Smoking and Drinking during last preg" (SMOKPREG)

If the respondent has never been pregnant(03/76-77 coded
00), then PREGSMOK is inapplicable, so PREGSMOK=BLANK.

If the respondent neither smoked nor drank during her
last pregnancy(14/66-67 coded 96 AND 14/68 coded 6),
then PREGSMOK=1.

If the respondent smoked but did not drink(14/66-67
coded 01-07 AND 14/68 coded 6), then PREGSMOK=2.

If the respondent drank but did not smoke(14/66-67 coded
96 AND 14/68 coded 1-5), then PREGSMOK=3.

If the respondent both smoked and drank during her
last pregnancy(14/66-67 coded 01-07 AND 14/68 coded
1-5), then PREGSMOK=4.

If there was missing data (codes 7, 8, or 9) in either
14/67 or 14/68, then PREGSMOK=9.

VAR360 "Fecundity Status" (FECUND)

If it is impossible for the respondent or her current
husband to have a(nother) baby because of a sterilizing
operation that was done because she had all the children
she wanted(16/21 coded 1,2,3,5,9 AND 16/33 coded 1) or:
(16/40 coded 1,2,3,5,9 AND 16/52=1) or (02/18 coded 1 or
4 AND 16/21 coded 4 AND 16/33 coded 1) or (02/18 coded

1 or 4 AND 16/40 coded 4 AND 16/52 coded 1), then she

is contraceptively sterile, so FECUND=1.

NOTE: As in the specs for Var 160, if the woman
or couple have had more than one sterilizing
operation(16/20 coded 2-5):

if one is a female and the other is a male
operation, classify using the female operation;

if both are female, or both are male, classify
using the earlier operation.

If it is impossible for the respondent or her current
husband to have a(nother) baby because of a sterilizing
operation that was done for noncontraceptive reasons((16/21 coded
32

1,2,3, 5, 9 AND 16/33 coded 2) or 16/40 coded 1,2,3,5,9 AND 16/52
coded 2) or (02/18 coded 1 or 4 AND 16/21 coded 4 AND 16/33 coded 2)
or (02/18 coded 1 or 4 AND 16/40 coded 4 AND 16/52 coded 2), then
she is surgically sterile for noncontraceptive reasons, so FECUND=2.

If 16/33=8 or 9, AND 16/18=2 AND 16/19=1 AND 16/20=1:

and if 16/21=2 or 4, then FECUND=1
and if 16/21=3, then FECUND=2

If it is impossible for the respondent to have a(nother)
baby for reasons other than surgical sterilization
(16/19 coded 2), then she is nonsurgically sterile, so FECUND=3.

If she reports that it is difficult for her, and/or her
current husband, to conceive or deliver a(nother) baby
(16/71 coded 1), or a medical doctor advised her never

to become pregnant(again) and said it would be dangerous
for her and/or the baby and she will have an operation

to be sure she won't become pregnant(16/75 coded 1), or

her husband will have an operation(16/76 coded 1), or

if she becomes pregnant (again) she will have the pregnancy
ended (16/77 coded 1), then she is subfecund, so FECUND =4.

If R is currently using the pill (TL 301=1), then R is
fecund, so FECUND=6.

If the respondent has used a method in the open interval
(13/18 coded 1), and if she is still using it (13/29-32 or
13/42-45 or 13/55-58 coded 9696), then R is fecund, so FECUND = 6.

If, during the 3 years of continuous marriage before
the interview, she did not conceive and used no contra-
ception--that is,

she is currently married(02/18 coded 1 or 4)and

the interval between the date of her current marriage
and the date of interview is 3 years or more(century
month in 26/49-54 minus century month in 20/53-58 GE 36);

and she has not become pregnant within the last 36 months
or more((04/39 coded 1 AND(26/49-54 minus 04/25-30 GE 36);

(IF SHE HAS NEVER BEEN PREGNANT(03/76-77 coded 00), then
skip the above test)

and she has not used a contraceptive method in the last

36 months or more(13/28 coded 2 AND 13/41 EQ 2 AND 13/54-EQ 2
AND 13/67 EQ 2) AND (take the last nonblank entry from
13/29-32, 13/42-45, 13/55-58, 13/68-71, and subtract it

from 26/49-54; the difference must be GE 36 months).

If R has never been pregnant(03/76-77 coded 00) and has

If R has been pregnant(03/76-77 coded 01-30) and never used

If R had missing data, then the case was assigned a value
after looking at a printout of the entire case. If there was
no useful information, then FECUND was assigned = 9 and imputed.

VAR370 "Nonintercourse in the open interval in last 12 months"

(NOSEX12 )

(1) If R never had intercourse, or never had intercourse
since first menstrual period(17/41 coded 2 or
08/33-37 coded 9595), then NOSEX12=96.

If R is currently pregnant(CONSTAT coded 20),
then she doesn't have an open interval, so
NOSEX12=95,

(2) If R has not had any periods of nonintercourse
(12/19-22 coded 9696) in the open interval, then
NOSEX12=0.

(3) For women reporting one or more periods of non-
intercourse in the open interval(12/19-22 not
equal to 9696,9797,9898, or 9999):
34

(4) if any of the months of nonintercourse
(12/19-26; 12/27-34; 12/35-42) fall within
the last 12 months--that is, date of interview
(26/49-52) MINUS ending date(12/23-26, or
12/31-34, or 12/39-42) is less than or
equal to 12 months--

then NOSEX12 is the NUMBER OF MONTHS within
the last 12 that the respondent did not
have intercourse at all.

If R has had intercourse only once (08/40 = 6),
then NOSEX12 equals the date of interview minus
the date of firt intercourse, or 12, whichever is
less.

NOTE: Analysts may wish, in studying currently married
women, to limit this to those married at least 12 months
before the interview date--that is, (25/49-52)
MINUS 20/53-56 GE 12 months. Looking at the
‘infertile’ category of INFERT (var 310) does this.

VAR372 "Nonintercourse in open interval in last 36 months" (NOSEX36)

In part 4 of the specifications for VAR370, use the last

VAR410 "Century Month of first live birth" (BABY1MO)

If the respondent has not had a live birth
(03/69-70 coded 00), then there is no date of
first live birth, so BABYIMO=BLANK.

If the respondent has had one or more live births
(03/69-70 coded 01-30), then the date of first
live birth is the earliest reported date(04/25-30),
of a live birth (04/18-19 coded 01,05,07).

VAR415 "Interval between first and second live birth" (BABY12)

If the respondent has never had a live birth(03/69-70
coded 00), then there is no interval from first to
second live birth, so BABY12=BLANK.

If the respondent had at least two pregnancies ending

in live birth(03/69-70 coded 02-30), then the interval

between the first and second live birth is the century

month of the second pregnancy ending in live birth

(BABY2M0) minus the century month of the first pregnancy ending
in live birth (BABY1MO0.

If the respondent has had only one pregnancy ending in
live birth(03/69-70 coded 01), then the interval is
the century month of interview (26/49-54) minus the
century month of the first pregnancy ending in live
birth(BABY1MO).

VAR420 "Central number of additional births expected" (ADDEXP)

If R is currently pregnant intending live birth
(03/75 coded 1 AND 03/26 coded 3 or 9), add 1 child
(times 10) to her additional number expected.

If the respondent does not intend to have more children
(17/18 coded 2), or if the questions are inapplicable,
(17/18 coded blank), then her additional number expected
is zero, so ADDEXP=00.

If she intends more(17/18 coded 1), and she knows her

additional intended number(17/19-22 NE 9797,9898, or 9999), then her
central additional number expected is the average of her

intended range, so ADDEXP= 10 times (17/19-20)+(17/21-22)

divided by 2.

If she disagrees with her husband about whether she intends
more children, or doesn’t know or thinks it's up to God —
(17/18 coded 3 or 8, or 17/19-22 coded 9797,9898, or 9999),
then her central additional number

expected is the average of her largest and smallest
(17/24-25 and 17/26-27) additional number expected, so
ADDEXP=10 times((17/24~-25) + 17/26-27) divided by 2.

VAR425 "Total Births Expected" (TOTEXP)

TOTEXP is the sum of ADDEXP (VAR 420) and PARITY (VAR 980)*10.
VAR428 “Number of wanted Pregnancies" (WTPREG)

WTPREG is the count of the number of pregnancies a
respondent has had that were wanted(the sum of codes
1,2,3, and 4 in (VAR 426, WANTWIFE).

VAR429 "Number of unwanted pregnancies" (UNWPREG)

UNWPREG is the count of the.number of pregnancies
a respondent has had that were unwanted
(the count of code 5 in VAR 426, WANTWIFE).

VAR430 "Number of wanted births" (WANTED)

WANTED is the count of the number of pregnancies

a respondent has had that ended in live birth(04/18-19=
01,05,07) AND that were wanted by the woman

(VAR 426, WANTWIFE=1,2,3,4).

VAR431_ “Number of timed births"(TIMED)

TIMED is the count of the number of pregnancies a
respondent has had that ended in live birth
(04/18-19=01,05, 07)AND that she wanted then,

sooner, or didn't care (VAR 426, WANTWIFE=1,2, or 4).

VAR432 "Number of mistimed births" (MISTIMED)

MISTIMED is the count of the number of pregnancies a
woman has had that ended in live birth
(04/18-19=01,05,07) AND that she wanted eventually,
but later than they occurred (VAR 426, WANTWIFE=3).

VAR433 "Number of unwanted births" (UNWANTED)

UNWANTED is the count of the number of pregnancies
a woman has had that ended in live birth
04/18-19=01,05,07) AND that she did not want ever
(WANTWIFE=5),

NOTE: VARS 434-443 are computed similarly, based on VAR 426,
WANTWIFE. VARS 444.452 are based on both WANTWIFE and
VAR 994 OUTCOME.

VAR505 "Age began dating" (DATE1AGE)
